explicit in the notation, for any
n\in[N]
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta),

(23)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Pi}_{t-1}^{N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{1},\theta),\dots,\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{N},\theta)\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta,{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\boldsymbol{\Pi}^{N}_{t-1}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,t|t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}^{N}_{n,t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,t|t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}^{N}_{n,t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right\}.

A.3
Assumptions

The compactness and continuity in the following assumption are standard conditions in the consistency theory of maximum likelihood estimators.

Assumption 6
.

The parameter space
\Theta
and the covariate space
\mathbb{W}
are compact subsets of Euclidean spaces. Moreover, the initial distribution
p_{0}(w,\theta)
, the transition matrix
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)
, and the emission matrix
G(w,\theta)
are all continuous functions in their arguments
w,\theta
.

For the purposes of our theory, we shall treat the individual-specific covariate vectors as random, independent, and identically distributed across the population. This can be interpreted as a random design assumption of a sort, which is commonly adopted in the asymptotic studies of regression and classification methods.

Assumption 7
.

The covariates
{\mathbf{w}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}_{2},\dots
are independent and identically distributed according to a distribution
\Gamma
on
\mathbb{W}
.

The following assumption will be used to establish that the logarithm of the CAL is well-defined across all possible values of parameters, covariates, and almost all realizations of the data.

Assumption 8
.

The following hold:

•

for any
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta
we have that
\textbf{supp}(p_{0}(w,\theta))=\textbf{supp}(p_{0}(w,\theta^{\prime}))
;

•

for any
w\in\mathbb{W}
,
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in[0,C]
and
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta
we have that
\textbf{supp}(K_{\eta}(w,\theta))=\textbf{supp}(K_{\eta^{\prime}}(w,\theta^{\prime}))
;

•

for any
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta
we have that
\textbf{supp}(G(w,\theta))=\textbf{supp}(G(w,\theta^{\prime}))
.

The following assumption constrains the form of the function
\eta
which determines the mechanism of interaction amongst the population.

Assumption 9
.

For any
\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},N\in\mathbb{N}
, and for any
W^{N}=(w_{1},\dots,w_{N}),\Pi^{N}=(\pi_{1},\dots,\pi_{N})
with
w_{n}\in\mathbb{W},\pi_{n}\in\Delta_{M}
for all
n\in[N]
, we have:

\eta^{N}(w,\theta,W^{N},\Pi^{N})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}d(w,w_{n},\theta)^{\top}\pi_{n},

(24)

where
d:\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{W}\times\Theta\to[0,C]^{M}
is a bounded function, i.e.
\left\lVert d\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C<\infty
, from which we also obtain
\eta^{N}(w,\theta,W^{N},\Pi^{N})\in[0,C]
for any
N,w,\pi,W^{N},\Pi^{N}
.

Assumption 10
.

For any
\theta\in\Theta
and
w\in\mathbb{W}
, the matrix
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)
is Lipschitz continuous in
\eta
with Lipschitz constant
L
, that is for any
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in[0,C]
we have:

\left\lVert K_{\eta}(w,\theta)-K_{\eta^{\prime}}(w,\theta)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq L\left\lvert\eta-\eta^{\prime}\right\rvert.

(25)

A.4
The data-generating process

All the random variables appearing in our theory are assumed to be defined on a common probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
. Thus in accordance with Assumption
7
, under
\mathbb{P}
,
{\mathbf{w}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}_{2},\ldots
are i.i.d.

Let
\theta^{\star}
be an arbitrarily chosen but a fixed member of
\Theta
, which will be referred to as the data-generating parameter (DGP). For each
N\geq 1
, the DGP determines the distributions of
({\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{N})_{t\geq 0}
and
({\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{N})_{t\geq 1}
under
\mathbb{P}
conditional on
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
, in that for each
n\in[N]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}^{N}_{n,0}|{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\,\cdot\,|\,p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right),

(26)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}|{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}^{N},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\,\cdot\,\left|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right.\right),

(27)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}|{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N},{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\,\cdot\,\left|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right.\right).

(28)

Appendix B
Closed-forms under categorical approximations

Proposition
2
and Proposition
3
below show how the formulae appearing in the CAL algorithm in Section
3.1
of the main paper are derived and can be interpreted as prediction and correction operations associated with the approximate model specified in Section
3.2
of the main paper.

Proposition 2
.

If
\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\bigotimes_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta))
and:

\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\right)\quad\text{for }n\in[N],

then
\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\bigotimes_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta))
with:

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)^{\top}\quad\text{for }n\in[N].

Proof.

We just want to compute the marginal at time
t
after applying the approximate transition kernel:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{X}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})\\
&=\sum_{X^{N}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}=X^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{X}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}=X^{N})\\
&=\sum_{X^{N}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})}\prod_{n\in[N]}\left[\left(x_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\left(x_{n}^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{x}_{n,t}\right)\right]\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x_{n}}\left[\left(x_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\left(x_{n}^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,\cdot)}\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N}.\end{split}

(29)

∎

The next proposition considers the correction step, where we perform a Bayes update with a factorized categorical distribution prior and a likelihood that is given by the emission distribution of the individual-based model. As the posterior distribution is in the family of the prior distribution, we say that the factorized categorical distribution is a conjugate prior for the individual-based model observation model.

Proposition 3
.

If
\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\bigotimes_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta))
and:

\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N},{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\right)\quad\text{for }n\in[N],

then
\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\bigotimes_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta))
with:

\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\quad\text{for }n\in[N],

(30)

and
\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\bigotimes_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta))
with:

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right\}\quad\text{for }n\in[N].

(31)

Proof.

We start by computing the marginal likelihood:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})&=\sum_{X^{N}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=X^{N})\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=X^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})\\
&=\sum_{X^{N}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})}\prod_{n\in[N]}\left(x_{n}^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\left(x_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x_{n}}\left(x_{n}^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\left(x_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left(G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,\cdot)}\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N},\end{split}

(32)

showing that the observations are marginally distributed as categorical distributions with the desired parameters. We can now compute the posterior distribution using Bayes theorem:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{X}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{N})=\frac{\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{X}_{t}^{N})\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{X}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})}{\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{N}=\widetilde{Y}_{t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N})}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\frac{\left((\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\frac{(\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\left(G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\odot\ \boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}(\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\left[\left(G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\odot\ \boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\oslash\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\frac{G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}(\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}}{\sum_{k\in[M]}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}(\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\frac{G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}}(\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\left\{\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]\widetilde{y}_{n,t}^{N}\right\}\right\}^{\top}\widetilde{x}_{n,t}^{N}.\end{split}

(33)

∎

As a consequence of Proposition
2
and
3
, we can see the CAL as an exact marginal likelihood for the approximate model:

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}^{N}_{n,0}|{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right),

(34)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}^{N}_{n,t}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{N}_{1:t-1},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\right),

(35)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}^{N}_{n,t}|\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}^{N}_{n,t},{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\right),

(36)

and we can compute both its joint likelihood and marginal likelihood, see the next proposition.

Proposition 4
.

Over a time horizon
T
, the marginal likelihood of
\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}
is given by:

p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\theta)\coloneqq\prod_{t=1}^{T}\prod_{n\in[N]}\mbox{Cat}\left(\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right),

(37)

which is the marginal of:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\theta)&\coloneqq\prod_{n\in[N]}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\\
&\qquad\cdot\prod_{t=1}^{T}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right],\end{split}

(38)

i.e.
\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T}^{N}}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\theta)=p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},\theta)
.

Proof.

For ease of presentation throughout the proof we omit conditioning on
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
from the notation. We prove the statement of the proposition by induction on
T
and start by showing that
p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1}^{N}|\theta)
satisfies the statement:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1}^{N}|\theta)&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N}=\prod_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{i,j,k}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,k)}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N})^{(k)}\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N}}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{1}^{N}}\prod_{n\in[N]}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{1}^{N}}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:1}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1}^{N}|\theta)\end{split}

(39)

for
\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t}^{N}=(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}^{N}_{1,t},\dots,\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}^{N}_{N,t})
with
t=0,1
, which complete the proof for the first time step. From the above we also get:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)&=\left[\sum\limits_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N}}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\odot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)^{\top}\right]\\
&\qquad\oslash\left[\sum\limits_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,1}^{N}}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\odot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)^{\top}\right]\\
&\qquad\odot\left(G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,1}^{N}\right).\end{split}

(40)

Now assume that the statement is valid for
T-1
:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)&=\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T-1}^{N}}\prod_{n\in[N]}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-1}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(41)

from which we also get:

\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,T-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\left\{\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-2}^{N}}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-2}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-2}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{T-2}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)^{\top}\right\}\\
&\qquad\oslash\left[\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-1}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\odot G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T-1}^{N}.\end{split}

(42)

Consider now time
T
:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|\theta)&=p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)\prod_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T}^{N}\\
&=p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)\prod_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T}^{N}\\
&=p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(43)

from which we remark that:

\begin{split}&(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,T-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\\
&=\left\{\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-2}^{N}}\left[(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-2}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-2}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{T-2}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N}\right)\right\}\\
&\qquad/\penalty\left[\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-1}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\right]\\
&\qquad\cdot(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T-1}^{N}.\end{split}

(44)

As from our inductive hypothesis we have:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-1}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(45)

which is the denominator in
(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
, we can conclude:

\begin{split}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:t}^{N}|\theta)&=p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T-1}^{N}|\theta)\\
&\quad\cdot\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right)\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T-2}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\prod_{t=1}^{T-2}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-2}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{T-2}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,T}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,T}^{N}\right)\\
&=\prod_{n\in[N]}\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0:T}^{N}}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,0}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot\prod_{t=1}^{T}\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\left((\widetilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\widetilde{{\mathbf{y}}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T}^{N}}p(\widetilde{{\mathbf{X}}}_{0:T}^{N},\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{1:T}^{N}|\theta),\end{split}

(46)

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Consistency of the maximum CAL estimator

With the definition:

\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)\coloneqq\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left[\left({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right],

(47)

our ultimate goal is to prove Theorem
33
, which establishes consistency of the maximum CAL estimator:

\begin{split}\hat{\theta}^{N}\coloneqq\argmax_{\theta\in\Theta}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta),\\
\end{split}

(48)

in the sense that
\hat{\theta}^{N}
converges to
\Theta^{\star}
as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, where
\Theta^{\star}\subset\Theta
is a set of parameter values which are, in a sense to be made precise, equivalent to the DGP
\theta^{\star}
.

The main steps are:

1.

prove almost sure pointwise in
\theta
convergence of
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)-\frac{1}{N}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta^{\star})
to a contrast function
\mathcal{C}(\theta,\theta^{\star})
, this is the subject of Theorem
29
;

2.

Use stochastic equi-continuity
Andrews (1992)
to prove that the almost sure convergence
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{N}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)-\frac{1}{N}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta^{\star})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\theta,\theta^{\star})
is uniform in
\theta
, this is the subject of Lemma
32
;

3.

prove Theorem
33
, and so the convergence of the maximum CAL estimator to a set of maximizers
\Theta^{\star}
;

4.

Characterize the set of maximizers of the contrast function
\Theta^{\star}
and prove that
\Theta^{\star}
contains
\theta^{\star}
; this is the subject of Theorem
29
and Lemma
35
.

Step 1. is by far the most complicated of the three. It involves proving
L^{4}
bounds for averages across the population in the data-generating process (Section
C.2
), averages of various quantities computed in the CAL algorithm (Section
C.3
), and comparison to averages across what we call the
saturated processes
and
saturated CAL algorithm
(Section
C.4
), which are processes we construct for purposes of our proofs in which members of the population are statistically decoupled. All of these ingredients are then combined to establish convergence to the contrast function (Theorem
29
).

C.1
Preliminaries

C.1.1
L^{4}
bound for conditionally independent random variables

We state and prove a result from
Whitehouse et al. (2023)
, which is useful to find
L^{4}
bounds of averages of random variables that are conditionally independent, bounded, and mean zero. This is going to be one of the main building blocks in our proof strategy.

Lemma 5
.

Consider a collection of random variable
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}
with
n\in[N]
. Assume that given a filtration
\mathcal{F}
the random variables
\boldsymbol{\delta}_{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\delta}_{N}
are conditionally independent, bounded by a constant
B<\infty
, i.e.
|\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}|\leq B
almost surely, and satisfy
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}|\mathcal{F}]=0
, then:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(49)

Proof.

From the Multinomial theorem we can see that:

\left(\sum_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}\right)^{4}=\sum\limits_{k_{1},\dots,k_{N}\in\mathbb{N}:k_{1}+\dots+k_{N}=4}\binom{4}{k_{1},\dots,k_{N}}\prod_{n\in[N]}(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n})^{k_{n}},

(50)

hence if we compute expectations with respect to the filtration we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}\right)^{4}\Big|\mathcal{F}\right]=\sum\limits_{k_{1},\dots,k_{N}\in\mathbb{N}:k_{1}+\dots+k_{N}=4}\binom{4}{k_{1},\dots,k_{N}}\prod_{n\in[N]}\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n})^{k_{n}}\big|\mathcal{F}\right],

(51)

because of the conditional independence assumption. Remark that we can combine a maximum of four terms as we are considering a power of 4 and we are constrained to
\sum_{n\in[N]}k_{n}=4
, meaning that we have only these possible combinations:

•

k_{i_{1}}+k_{i_{2}}+k_{i_{3}}+k_{i_{4}}=4
;

•

k_{i_{1}}+k_{i_{2}}+k_{i_{3}}=4
;

•

k_{i_{1}}+k_{i_{2}}=4
;

•

k_{i_{1}}=4
;

with all the other
k
’s being zero. Given that
\mathbb{E}[\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}|\mathcal{F}]=0
then all the combinations which involve a
k_{i}=1
can be safely removed, we then end up with:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{n\in[N]}\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n}\right)^{4}\Big|\mathcal{F}\right]&=\sum\limits_{n\in[N]}\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n})^{4}\Big|\mathcal{F}\right]+\binom{4}{2,2}\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in[N],n\neq n^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n})^{2}|\mathcal{F}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[(\boldsymbol{\delta}_{n^{\prime}})^{2}|\mathcal{F}\right]\\
&\leq\sum\limits_{n\in[N]}(B)^{4}+6\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in[N],n\neq n^{\prime}}(B)^{2}(B)^{2}\\
&=NB^{4}+6N(N-1)B^{4}\\
&=B^{4}(N+6N^{2}-6N)\leq 6B^{4}N^{2},\end{split}

(52)

from which the statement of the Lemma follows by the tower rule, dividing by
N^{4}
and exponentiating by
\frac{1}{4}
.
∎

C.1.2
Checking the CAL is almost surely well-defined

If
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=0
, then (
47
) would evaluate to
\log(0)
. The aim of this section is to prove that the CAL is almost surely well-defined, in the sense that
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=0
happens with zero probability no matter the values of
N
and
\theta
.

The main result is Theorem
10
, and its proof builds upon propositions
6
-
9
below, which exploit the recursive nature of the CAL algorithm and the data-generating process.

Proposition 6
.

Under Assumption
8
, for any
n\in[N]
if there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
p_{0}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
then
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}=0
,
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N})
-almost surely.

Proof.

Under Assumption
8
we have that
p_{0}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
p_{0}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(i)}=0
, hence:

\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)=p_{0}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(i)}=0,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7
.

Under Assumption
8
, for any
t\geq 1
and
n\in[N]
if there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(i)}=0
,
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
-almost surely, then, there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
,
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
-almost surely.

Proof.

Note that for
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
the following are equivalent:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0&\iff\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}(w_{n},\theta)}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\\
&\iff\forall j\in[M]\quad\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}(w_{n},\theta)}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0.\end{split}

(53)

We then have that for any
j
either:

1.

\mathbb{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}=0
which implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
by assumption, or

2.

K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}(w_{n},\theta)}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
, which implies that there exists
\eta=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}(w_{n},\theta)
such that
K_{\eta}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
. Then by Assumption
8
we have that
K_{\eta}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
for all
\eta\in[0,C]
and
\theta\in\Theta
meaning
K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
,
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
-almost surely as it holds for almost any realization of
{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}\in\mathbb{O}_{M}^{N}\subset\Delta_{M}^{N}
, where we remark that
{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}
appears in
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})=\eta^{N}(w_{n},\theta^{\star},W^{N},{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N})
.

Now given that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(54)

and by defining
{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}
as
{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}
with the
n
-th individual removed, we can notice that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}}\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}=X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\quad\sum_{x_{n,t-1}^{N}}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}=x_{n,t-1}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}=X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\quad\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N}=X_{t-1}^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}}\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}=X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\quad\sum_{j}\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(j)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{{\mathbf{X}}}_{t-1}^{N\setminus n}=X_{t-1}^{N\setminus n},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}\\
&=0,\end{split}

(55)

where the last step follows from the fact that for all
j
we either have
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
, or
K_{\eta}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
for any
\eta\in[0,C]
and so
K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})}(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
for any
X_{t-1}^{N}
. As
\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=0
we can conclude
\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=1
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8
.

Under Assumption
8
, for any
t\geq 1
and
n\in[N]
if there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
, then:

•

there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
;

•

there exists
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N})
.

Proof.

Let us start with the results regarding the observations. Note that for
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
the following are equivalent:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0&\iff\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\\
&\iff\forall j\in[M]\quad\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0.\end{split}

(56)

We then have that for all
j
either:

1.

\mathbb{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}=0
which implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
by assumption, or

2.

G(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
which implies
G(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
because of Assumption
8
.

Now given that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(57)

and:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{x_{n,t}^{N}}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}=x_{n,t}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\quad\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}=x_{n,t}^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{j}\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)G(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0,\end{split}

(58)

where the last step follows from the fact that for all
j
we either have
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
or
G(w_{n},\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
. As

\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=0,

we can conclude

\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=1,

which concludes the proof of the first part. As a consequence if there exists
j\in[M]
such that
\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})>0
then
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0
, meaning that

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)=1.

(59)

Consider now
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}
, and observe that:

\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0\iff\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\frac{\sum_{j}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}}{\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}}=0\\
&\iff\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=0\text{ and }\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0.\end{split}

(60)

From the (
59
), we know that under
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
we have that the denominator
\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}
is almost surely different from
0
hence:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=0\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\text{ and }\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right),\end{split}

(61)

as we are considering an intersection with an almost sure event.

We then just need to prove
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=0
almost surely. We have either:

•

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
, implying
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
because we consider this statement to be true, or

•

\sum_{j}G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}=0
, which tells us that there exists
k\in[M]
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)>0
and
G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,k)}=0
, note that under
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N})
we know
k
as we are conditioning on
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
.

Now given that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N})=1-\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N}),\end{split}

(62)

we can notice that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N})\\
&\propto\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1,({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})\\
&=G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,k)}\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})=0,\end{split}

(63)

because we have either
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
or
G(w_{n},\theta)^{(i,k)}=0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

We can now combine Proposition
6
, Proposition
7
, and Proposition
8
in the following proposition.

Proposition 9
.

Under Assumption
8
, for any
t\geq 1
,
n\in[N]
, the following hold:

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
;

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N})
;

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t}^{N}=Y_{1:t}^{N})
.

Proof.

Suppose that the third statement is true at time
t-1
, which is valid at
t-1=0
because of Proposition
6
, which guarantees that if
\exists\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]:\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=0\implies({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N})
. We can in turn apply Proposition
7
to prove the first statement and Proposition
8
to prove the second and the third for
t
. As the third statement is our inductive hypothesis at the next time step
t
we can then close the induction and conclude that the three statements are valid for an arbitrary
t
.
∎

We finally prove the main result.

Theorem 10
.

Under assumptions
7
,
8
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N},t\geq 1,n\in[N]
and
\theta\in\Theta
we have that
\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}
.

Proof.

We can see that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]:\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]:\quad\forall i\in[M]\quad\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N],k\in[M]:\quad\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}=0\text{ and }({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right),\end{split}

(64)

where the second equality uses the fact that
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
is a one-hot encoding vector, i.e. the
k
th component is
1
while the others are
0
. Moreover:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]:\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0\right|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)\\
&=\sum_{Y_{1:t-1}^{N}}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N],k\in[M]:\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}=0\text{ and }({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\quad\cdot\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)=0,\end{split}

(65)

as from the second statement of Proposition
9
we know that event
\left\{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}=0\right\}\cap\left\{({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1\right\}
is probability zero conditionally on
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}
, indeed:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N],k\in[M]:\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}=0\text{ and }({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N],k\in[M]:\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{if }\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(k)}=0\text{ then }({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(k)}=0|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}=Y_{1:t-1}^{N}\right)\\
&=0.\end{split}

(66)

We can then conclude the proof of the first statement as:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0\right|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)\\
&=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\exists\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]:\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}=0\right|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)=1.\end{split}

(67)

To conclude the proof we need:

\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0\right)=1,

(68)

which can be proven by observing that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0\right)\\
&=\int\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,n\in[N]\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(w_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0\right|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}=W^{N}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\Gamma(dw_{1})\dots\Gamma(dw_{N})\\
&=1,\end{split}

(69)

where we applied Assumption
7
and where the last step follows from what we have just proven.
∎

C.1.3
Checking the CAL is almost surely bounded

In this section, we want to prove that all the non-zero elements of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
are almost surely bounded below by a quantity
{m}_{t}>0
that does not depend on
N
, this will be put to use in establishing
L^{4}
bounds in Section
C.3
.

Proposition 11
.

Under assumptions
6
,
8
,
10
, for
t\geq 1
there exists
{m}_{t}>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in[N]
we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Proof.

For a fixed
N\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the following inductive hypothesis. There exists
\bar{m}_{t-1}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall n\in[N],\theta\in\Theta.

We start by proving that the inductive hypothesis is true when
t-1=0
. From Assumption
6
we have that
p_{0}(w,\theta)
is continuous in
w,\theta
and both
\mathbb{W}
and
\Theta
are compact, we then get from Weierstrass theorem that there exists a minimum
m_{0}
such that for any realization of
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
and for any
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\right)
:

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}=p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\right)}p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\eqqcolon m_{0},

with
m_{0}>0
as we are considering a minimum over
j\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\right)
which excludes all the zeros. As
m_{0}
does not depend on
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
we conclude that there exists
m_{0}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq m_{0}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall n\in[N],\theta\in\Theta.

Let us now work on a general time step
t
. For
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)
,

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&=\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&=\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(70)

where the inequality holds
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely by the inductive hypothesis. Several other inequalities in the remainder of the proof hold
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, but to avoid repetition we do not state this explicitly. As:

\displaystyle i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\neq 0,

(71)

we can conclude that there exists at least one component of
K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}
which is different from zero, hence:

\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\geq\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}>0.

(72)

Because of Assumption
8
we have:

K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\Longleftrightarrow K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\quad\forall\eta\in[0,C],

meaning that

\begin{split}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}&K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\geq\min_{\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}.\end{split}

(73)

We can then conclude:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\min_{\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\min_{\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)\right)}K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}.\end{split}

(74)

Note that
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)
is continuous in
\eta
because of Assumption
10
and also in
w,\theta
because of Assumption
6
. Furthermore, the domain of
\eta
is
[0,C]
, which does not depend on
N
and it is compact. Additionally,
\mathbb{W},\Theta
are compact by Assumption
6
. Hence we can conclude by the Weirstrass theorem that there exist
\eta_{min}\in[0,C]
,
w_{min}\in\mathbb{W},(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)\right)
, and
\theta_{min}\in\Theta
such that:

m_{K}\coloneqq K_{\eta_{min}}(w_{min},\theta_{min})^{(j,i)}=\min_{\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)\right)}K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}>0,

(75)

where strict positivity follows from the observation made in Equation (
72
). Hence:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}>0,\end{split}

(76)

where the lower bounding constants do not depend on
{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:t-1}^{N}
,
\theta
or
N
. Therefore we can conclude that there exist
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Similarly, for
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)
,

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&=\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(77)

where the inequality follows from what we have proven above. Moreover:

\displaystyle i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\neq 0,

(78)

meaning that there exists at least one component of
G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}
which is different from zero. Hence following the same reasoning as above:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(G(w,\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(G(w,\theta)\right)}G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(79)

and as
G(w,\theta)
is continuous in
w,\theta
because of Assumption
6
and
\mathbb{W},\Theta
are compact because of Assumption
6
we can conclude by Weirstrass theorem that there exists a minimum
m_{G}
such that:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}>0,\end{split}

(80)

where the strict inequality follows from considering a minimum on the support of matrix
G
.

We conclude that there exist
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K},m_{G}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Consider
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)
then:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\frac{\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}}{\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)},\end{split}

(81)

where the inequality follows from what we have proven above, from
\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\leq 1
by definition and
\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely because of Theorem
10
. We now observe that:

\displaystyle i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\neq 0,

(82)

meaning that there is at least one element of the
G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,\cdot)}
which is different from zero. Following the same reasoning of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}
we can conclude:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,\cdot)}\right)}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(G(w,\theta)\right)}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}>0.\end{split}

(83)

We can conclude that there exist constants
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K},m_{G}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1,\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

We can then set
\bar{m}_{t}\coloneqq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}
and conclude that there exists
\bar{m}_{t}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1,\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta,

which closes the induction, meaning that the above equality holds for an arbitrary
t
. As a consequence, we also have that for any
t\geq 1
there exists
{m}_{t}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1,\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta,

with
{m}_{t}\coloneqq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}
, which concludes the proof.

∎

C.2
L^{4}
bounds for averages of the data-generating process

In Section
C.2
we establish
L^{4}
bounds for averages across the population of disease states and observations.

Initial condition.

We start by considering the population at
t=0
.

Proposition 12
.

Under Assumption
7
, there exists
\alpha_{0}\geq 0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
, i.e.
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B<\infty
,

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{0}.

(84)

Moreover, there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}\geq 0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
,

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}.

(85)

Proof.

For the first statement note that by Minkowski inequality:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(86)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(87)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(88)

Starting from (
87
), which is also the second statement, we observe that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]&=\sum_{i=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{M}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)^{(i)}=f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right),\end{split}

(89)

moreover:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\right\rvert\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}\right\rvert+\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)^{(i)}\right\rvert\right]\\
&\leq B\sum_{i=1}^{M}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})^{(i)}+p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)^{(i)}\right]=2B,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely},\end{split}

(90)

as
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}
is a one-hot encoding vector and
p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)
is a probability distribution for any
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
. As
f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)
are also conditionally independent across
n
given the population covariates
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
because of the factorization of the initial distribution, we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(91)

which also proves the second statement for
\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}=1
.

Similarly for (
88
) we have:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\right]&=\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw),\end{split}

(92)

and also:

\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\right\rvert\leq 2B,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely}.

As
f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)
are independent because functions of independent random variables, indeed
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
are i.i.d. samples from
\Gamma
, we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(93)

By putting everything together we conclude the proof by setting
\alpha_{0}=2
, indeed:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(94)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(95)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}p_{0}\left({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star}\right)-\int f(w)^{\top}p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(96)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}=2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}2.

(97)

∎

Dynamics.

We now turn to the behavior of
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}
, for
t\geq 1
. We need the following definitions, for
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq p_{0}\left(w,\theta^{\star}\right)

(98)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq\int d(w,\widetilde{w},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\widetilde{w},\theta^{\star})\Gamma(d\widetilde{w})

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}.

The quantity
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}
can be loosely interpreted as being
\eta^{N}(\cdot,\cdot,{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{X}}^{N}_{t-1})
but with
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
and
{\mathbf{X}}^{N}_{t-1}
integrated out in the
N\to\infty
limit.

Proposition 13
.

Under assumptions
7
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\alpha_{t}>0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t}.

(99)

Moreover, under assumptions
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}>0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
,

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}.

(100)

Proof.

We prove the first statement by induction on
t
and start by assuming that there exists
\alpha_{t-1}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
we have:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1},

(101)

which is true at the initial time step under
\alpha_{0}=2
because of the first statement of Proposition
12
.

We then look at time step
t
and decompose the problem into three sub-problems:

\begin{split}&f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\\
&=f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\\
&+f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\\
&+f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw).\end{split}

(102)

By the Minkowski inequality, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(103)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(104)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(105)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}

(106)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(107)

Consider (
104
), we can notice that:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}|{\mathbf{X}}^{N}_{t-1},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]&=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(j)}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}|{\mathbf{X}}^{N}_{t-1},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{(j)}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(j)}\\
&=f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}.\end{split}

(108)

Given that we are considering differences of scalar products of
f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})
with probability/one-hot encoding vectors, we have:

\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right\rvert\leq 2B\quad\mathbb{P}-\text{almost surely}.

(109)

As
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}
are conditionally independent across
n
given the population covariates and the state of the population at the previous time step we can apply Lemma
5
and get:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(110)

We now consider (
105
) and note that:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right\rvert\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\lvert({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{(i)}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{(i,j)}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(j)}\right\rvert\\
&\leq B\left\lVert K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq BL\left\lvert\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right\rvert,\end{split}

(111)

where the final steps follow from the boundedness of
f
, the definition of one-hot encoding vectors, and the Lipschitz-continuity assumption on the transition matrix stated in Assumption
10
. We now use the structure of
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{n,t-1}
given in Assumption
9
:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right\rvert\\
&=\left\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta^{\star})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}^{N}_{k,t-1}-\int d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta^{\star})\Gamma(d{\mathbf{w}})\right\rvert,\end{split}

(112)

hence we get the following bound for (
105
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{BL}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta^{\star})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}^{N}_{k,t-1}-\int d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},w,\theta^{\star})\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.\end{split}

(113)

As for almost any realization
w_{n}\in\mathbb{W}
of
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
the function
d(w_{n},\cdot)
is a bounded function because of Assumption
9
we can apply our inductive hypothesis and get:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d(w_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta^{\star})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}^{N}_{k,t-1}-\int d(w_{n},w,\theta^{\star})\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2C\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}.\end{split}

(114)

Since the above bound holds for any
w_{n}\in\mathbb{W}
, the same inequality holds
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely when the random covariate
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
is substituted in place of
w_{n}
. We then conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{BL}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta^{\star})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}^{N}_{k,t-1}-\int d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},w,\theta^{\star})\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{BL}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}2C\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}=2BLC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}.\end{split}

(115)

where the first step follows from Minkowski inequality, the second one from our previous calculations, and the final one from the inductive assumption and the fact that
\left\lVert d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely from Assumption
9
.

The last term that is left to bound is (
107
). Given that it can be easily proven that for any row-stochastic matrix
K
the function
Kf(\cdot)
given by
{\mathbf{w}}\mapsto Kf({\mathbf{w}})
is such that
\left\lVert Kf(\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}
, we can conclude that
\left\lVert K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(\cdot,\theta^{\star})}(\cdot,\theta^{\star})f(\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
. We can then apply our inductive assumption and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\int\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})f(w)\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}.\end{split}

(116)

By putting everything together we have:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n}){\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2BLC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[1+(1+LC)\alpha_{t-1}\right],\end{split}

(117)

from which we can conclude the proof of the first statement by setting
\alpha_{t}=\left[1+(1+LC)\alpha_{t-1}\right]
.

The proof of the second statement follows similarly by induction on
t
. Start by assuming that there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
for any
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
bounded function such that for time
t-1
we have:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1},

(118)

which is true at the initial time step under
\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}=1
because of the second statement of Proposition
12
. We then follow the same steps as the previous proof, but we substitute (
107
) with:
