\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}\left[{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N}-\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1},\end{split}

(119)

which is bounded because of the induction hypothesis. We can then conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2BC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[1+C\alpha_{t-1}+\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1}\right],\end{split}

(120)

from which we can conclude the proof of the second statement by setting
\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}=\left[1+C\alpha_{t-1}+\widetilde{\alpha}_{t-1}\right]
.
∎

The following corollary tells us that in the large population limit, the interaction term
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})
converges to the quantity
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})
defined in (
98
) which depends on individual-specific covariate
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
, but not on the covariates or diseases states of the rest of the population. This can be interpreted as meaning that individuals become statistically decoupled as the population size grows.

Corollary 14
.

Under assumptions
7
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\alpha_{t-1}>0
such that:

\Bigg\vvvert\boldsymbol{\eta}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2C\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}.

(121)

Proof.

The result is a byproduct of the proof of Proposition
13
. In particular, the passage proving the bound on Equation (
112
) proves exactly the statement of the present corollary.
∎

Observations.

Using Proposition
13
, we next establish an
L^{4}
bound for averages across the population of observations. We need the following definition, for
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
t\geq 1
:

\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}G(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}.

(122)

Proposition 15
.

Under assumptions
7
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\alpha_{t}>0
such that for any bounded
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M+1}

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right).

(123)

Moreover, under assumptions
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}>0
such that for bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M+1}
:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}\right).

(124)

Proof.

To prove the first statement, the first step is to note that by Minkowski inequality:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\quad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}G(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(125)

as by definition
\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)=\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}G(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw)
. For the first term, we notice that:

\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\Big|{\mathbf{X}}_{t}^{N},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]=f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]=\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N},

(126)

meaning that the arguments of the sum are all conditionally independent and with mean zero. Moreover, given that
[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})]^{\top}
is a probability vector and
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
is a one-hot encoding vector, we can also conclude that the arguments of the sums are all bounded by
2B
, hence we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(127)

For the second term we just need some refactoring:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}G(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int\left[G(w,\theta^{\star})f(w)\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.\end{split}

(128)

As
\left\lVert G(\cdot,\theta^{\star})f(\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}
because
G(\cdot,\theta^{\star})
is a row-stochastic matrix, we can just apply the first statement of Proposition
13
and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-\int f(w)^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}G(w,\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t}.\end{split}

(129)

We then conclude the proof by putting everything together:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-\int f(w)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right).\end{split}

(130)

Given the above proof strategy, it is trivial to establish the second statement by simply substituting the last term of the Minkowski inequality with:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(131)

and by applying the second statement of Proposition
13
.
∎

C.3
L^{4}
bounds for the CAL filtering algorithm

The aim of this section is to establish
L^{4}
bounds for averages across the population of the various probability vectors computed in the CAL filtering recursion, (
23
). In Proposition
16
and Proposition
18
below, we shall see that the large-population behavior of these averages is determined by the following quantities.
Specifically, for
w\in\mathbb{W}
:

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq p_{0}(w,\theta),

(132)

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\int d(w,\widetilde{w},\theta)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\widetilde{w},\theta)\Gamma(d\widetilde{w}),

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)}(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}G(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G(w,\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right\},

where
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
is defined in (
122
).

Proposition 16
.

Under Assumption
7
, for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
it holds that:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)\Gamma(d{\mathbf{w}})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(133)

Moreover, for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}=0.

(134)

Proof.

The first statement follows the same reasoning of Proposition
12
, but we include it here for completeness. As:

\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]=\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw),

and we have a sequence of independent and bounded random variables:

\left\lvert f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\right\rvert\leq 2B,

we can conclude the proof by applying Lemma
5
. The second statement follows trivially from the definition of the limiting process, indeed
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
.
∎

Proposition 17
.

If there exists a constant
\gamma_{t-1}>0
such that for any
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t-1},

(135)

then under assumptions
9
,
10
there exists
\gamma_{t|t-1}>0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
,

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1}.

(136)

Moreover, under assumptions
7
,
9
,
10
, for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
it also holds:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw))\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\gamma_{t|t-1}+1\right).

(137)

Proof.

As a preliminary notice that:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1),\end{split}

(138)

as we can apply Minkowski inequality and then bound the first quantity with our assumption at time
t-1
and the second quantity with Lemma
5
, as we are dealing with an average of independent random variables that are mean zero and bounded (this follows the same steps as the proof of Proposition
16
).

As a consequence, we have the following implication of our assumption:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1),

(139)

and we know that if we prove the first statement it is enough to follow the above reasoning to prove the second one.

We now start the proof of the first statement by noting:

\begin{split}&f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\\
&=f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\\
&+f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}.\end{split}

(140)

Hence we can apply Minkowski inequality:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(141)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(142)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(143)

On (
142
) we can apply Assumption
10
and get:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{B}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(144)

which follows the same steps as the proof of Proposition
13
. Again, similarly to Proposition
13
, we can apply Assumption
10
on the Lipschitz continuity of the transition matrix, Assumption
9
on the structure of
\eta^{N}
and, given the definition of
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t}^{N}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t}
, we get:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{k,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta)-\int d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},w,\theta)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2LC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1),\end{split}

(145)

because of the inductive assumption, and which holds
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely following the same reasoning of Proposition
13
. Hence:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{B}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta)^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{k,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{k},\theta)-\int d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},w,\theta)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{B}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}2LC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1)=2BLC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1),\end{split}

(146)

where the last step follows from (
138
) and from the same reasoning of Proposition
13
, i.e. we prove the inequality
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

On (
143
), we can observe that the vectorial function
{\mathbf{w}}\mapsto K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}})
is bounded, i.e.
\left\lVert K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(\cdot,\theta)}(\cdot,\theta)f\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
, as
K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)}
is a row-stochastic matrix, hence we can apply our assumption on the time step
t-1
and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t-1}.\end{split}

(147)

By putting everything together we get:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2BLC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1)+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t-1}=2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[LC(\gamma_{t-1}+1)+\gamma_{t-1}\right],\end{split}

(148)

by setting
\gamma_{t|t-1}=\left[C(\gamma_{t-1}+1)+\gamma_{t-1}\right]
we conclude the proof of the first statement.

Remark that as we already mentioned at the beginning of the proof we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]=\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw),

(149)

and the random variables
f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)
are all bounded by
2B
and independent, hence by Lemma
5
:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(150)

which proves the second statement.
∎

Proposition 18
.

There exists
\gamma_{t|t-1}>0
for any
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
bounded function such that

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1},

(151)

then under assumptions
6
,
8
,
9
,
10
for any
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
bounded function we have:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1},

(152)

and there exists
\gamma_{t}>0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
,

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t}.

(153)

Moreover, under assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
, for any
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}
bounded function

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\gamma_{t|t-1}),

(154)

and:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1+\gamma_{t}).

(155)

Proof.

The first statement is straightforward, indeed:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\right]^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.\end{split}

(156)

As
G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)
is a row-stochastic matrix the function
{\mathbf{w}}\mapsto G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)f({\mathbf{w}})
is bounded, and precisely
\left\lVert G(\cdot,\theta)f(\cdot)\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
, meaning that the proof of the statement follows simply by the assumption on step
t-1
:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1}.\end{split}

(157)

The proof of the second statement is more involved and we need to start by reformulating
f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
:

\begin{split}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)&=\sum_{i=1}^{M}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{M}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{(i)}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i,j)}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j)}}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(j)}\\
&=\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right],\end{split}

(158)

where we define
G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)
as the matrix with elements
G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{(i,j)}=\frac{G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{(i,j)}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}
where
\frac{0}{0}=0
by convention. Remark that, because of Theorem
10
, the CAL is well-defined
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Also a similar reformulation to the one in (
158
) can be done on
\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)
meaning:

f({\mathbf{w}})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)=\left[f({\mathbf{w}})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta^{\star})\right]
.
Then we note that:

\begin{split}&f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\\
&=\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad+\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad+\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right].\end{split}

(159)

With the above decomposition we can apply Minkowski inequality and conclude:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(160)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]

(161)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(162)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]

(163)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(164)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]

(165)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(166)

Starting from (
162
), we notice that:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(167)

as
G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
is a vector of probabilities, i.e. elements that are less or equal than 1, we can conclude that:

\left\lVert f(\cdot)\odot G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}(\cdot,\theta)}(\cdot,\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B,\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely},

hence, similarly to what we do with
d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\cdot)
in the proof of Proposition
13
, we can apply our assumption on time step
t-1
for almost any realization of
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
, and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1}.\end{split}

(168)

As we define
G_{\mu}
as the matrix with elements
G^{(i,j)}/\penalty\mu^{(j)}
, we can notice that given two vectors
a,b
with the same dimensions of
G_{\mu}
we have:

x^{\top}G_{\mu}b-x^{\top}G_{\widetilde{\mu}}b=\sum_{i,j}x^{(i)}y^{(j)}\frac{G^{(i,j)}\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}-G^{(i,j)}\mu^{(j)}}{\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}{\mu}^{(j)}}=\sum_{i,j}x^{(i)}\frac{y^{(j)}}{\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}{\mu}^{(j)}}G^{(i,j)}\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}-\mu^{(j)}\right)

(169)

Hence we can reformulate (
164
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left\{\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}^{\top}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\\
&\qquad\qquad\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(170)

from which we can notice that for any
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
:

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\left\{\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\rVert_{\infty},\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely},\end{split}

(171)

where the first step follows from
\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)=\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)\right]^{\top}
and the elementwise ratio
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
is well-defined because of Theorem
10
.

Because of Proposition
11
we know that there exists
{m}_{t}>0
such that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\geq{m}_{t}
almost surely. We can then conclude that for any
n\in[N]
:

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\left\{\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\frac{\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}}{{m}_{t}}\leq\frac{B}{{m}_{t}},\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely}.\end{split}

(172)

Hence we can apply to (
164
) the same reasoning that we do with
d({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\cdot)
in the proof of Proposition
13
and apply (
157
) for almost any realization of
{\mathbf{Y}}^{N}_{1:t-1},{\mathbf{y}}^{N}_{n,t}
, i.e.
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\gamma_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}.\end{split}

(173)

Finally, we notice that for (
166
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\qquad=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left\{\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}-\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(174)

from which we can see that:

\begin{split}\left\lVert\left[f(\cdot)\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)\right]^{\top}G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)}(\cdot,\theta)\right\rVert_{\infty}&\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert\left[\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)\right]^{\top}G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)}(\cdot,\theta)\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert\sum_{i}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)^{(i)}\frac{G(\cdot,\theta)^{(i,j)}}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)^{(j)}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&=\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{\infty},\end{split}

(175)

where the last step follows from
\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta),G(\cdot,\theta),\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)
being almost surely positive and from
[\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)=\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\theta)^{\top}G(\cdot,\theta)]^{\top}
. We can then apply Proposition
15
and conclude

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\odot\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right).\end{split}

(176)

By putting everything together we then have:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\gamma_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right)\\
&=2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[\gamma_{t|t-1}+\frac{\gamma_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}+\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right)\right],\end{split}

(177)

which concludes the proof for
\gamma_{t}=\left[\gamma_{t|t-1}+\frac{\gamma_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}+\left(1+\alpha_{t}\right)\right]
.

The third and the fourth statements are simple consequences of the first and the second statement, Minkowski inequality and Lemma
5
, see the reasoning to prove the second statement of Proposition
17
.
∎

We now combine propositions
16
-
18
.

Proposition 19
.

Under assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exist
\gamma_{t|t-1}>0
and
\gamma_{t}>0
such that for any bounded function
f:\mathbb{W}\to[0,B]^{M}

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1},

(178)

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t|t-1},

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f({\mathbf{w}}_{n})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\int f(w)^{\top}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\Gamma(dw)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{t}.

Proof.

Assume that the third statement is true at time
t-1
, which is verified for
t-1=0
because of Proposition
16
. Then we can in turn apply Proposition
17
to prove the first statement, and Proposition
18
to prove the second and the third. As the third statement is also our inductive hypothesis and we have proven it to hold for the next time step
t
we can state that the three statements hold for an arbitrary time step
t
.
∎

The following corollary is analogous to Corollary
14
, but addresses the quantity
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}(\cdot,\cdot)
computed in the CAL algorithm (recall
23
). Corollary
20
can be interpreted as meaning that the probability vectors computed in the CAL algorithm become decoupled across the population as
N\to\infty
. Recall from (
98
) and (
132
) the definitions of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Corollary 20
.

Under assumptions
7
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\gamma_{t-1}>0
such that for any
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
\theta\in\Theta
,

\Bigg\vvvert\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}(w,\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2LC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1).

(179)

Moreover,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})=\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})
.

Proof.

The first statement is a byproduct of Proposition
19
and Equation (
145
) in the proof of Proposition
17
. Indeed, Proposition
19
ensures that the assumptions of Proposition
17
hold.

The second statement follows by induction, indeed
\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
by definition hence
\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
holds for
t=1
. Suppose now
\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
is true, then we get that for any
i\in[M]
:

\begin{split}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)}&=\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)}\left\{\left[G(w,\theta^{\star})\oslash\left(1_{M}\bar{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}\right)\right]\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right\}^{(i)}\\
&=\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)}\left\{\left[G(w,\theta^{\star})\oslash\left(1_{M}{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{\top}\right)\right]\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right\}^{(i)}\\
&=\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\frac{G(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i,j)}}{\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j)}}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j)}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\bar{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j)}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=\boldsymbol{\lambda}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)},\end{split}

(180)

which concludes the proof.
∎

C.4
The saturated processes and saturated CAL algorithm

In Section
C.4
we introduce key objects which will allow us to understand the large-population behavior of the data-generating process and the CAL algorithm. The first is what we call the
one-individual saturated process
. This process can be interpreted as describing the evolution of a single individual in an infinite population, where the covariate vector associated with this one individual is drawn from the distribution
\Gamma
appearing in Assumption
7
. The term “saturated” refers to the fact that the law of the process is defined in terms of the limiting interaction terms
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
,
t\geq 0
, cf. Corollary
14
.

We then introduce the
population saturated process
, which consists of independent individuals, each of which is distributed in the same way as the one-individual saturated process, but with covariate vectors
({\mathbf{w}}_{n})_{n\in[N]}
which are shared with those in the data-generating process specified in Section
A.4
. In Proposition
24
we shall show that averages across the population in the data-generating process approximate averages across the population saturated process.

Lastly, we introduce the
saturated CAL algorithm
, which is very similar to the CAL algorithm, but uses a model that is decoupled across individuals. Proposition
27
below will tell us that the logarithm of the CAL approximates the logarithm of the corresponding quantity from the saturated CAL algorithm.

One-individual saturated process.

We define the one-individual saturated process as:

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\Gamma,\\
&{\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\,\cdot\,|\,p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right),\\
&{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\,\cdot\,\left|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right.\right),\\
&{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot\left|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right.\right).\end{split}

(181)

Conditional on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
, the joint process
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 0}
,
({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 1}
described in (
181
) is a HMM. If in Recursion (
182
) below
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
and
\theta^{\star}
are substituted in place of
w
and
\theta
, then (
182
) becomes the forward algorithm associated with this HMM.

Recall from (
132
) the definition of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
, and define for
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
t\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq p_{0}(w,\theta),

(182)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)}(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}G(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G(w,\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}\right\}.

Recall from (
182
) the definition of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Proposition 21
.

Over a time horizon
T
, let
w\in\mathbb{W}
and
y_{1:T}\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}
:

p({\mathbf{y}}_{1:T}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\coloneqq\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)=\prod_{t=1}^{T}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star}),

(183)

and it is obtained as a marginal distribution over
x_{0:T}\in\mathbb{O}_{M}
of:

\begin{split}&p({\mathbf{x}}_{0:T}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:T}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\coloneqq\\
&\mbox{Cat}\left({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{0}|p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t}|\left[({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t-1})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left({\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{t}|\left[({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right)\\
&=\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\infty})^{\top}p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)\prod_{t=1}^{T}\left[\left(({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t-1})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star}){\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t}\right)\left(({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{t})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star}){\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{t}\right)\right].\end{split}

(184)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right),

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right).

Proof.

Note that because of Corollary
20
we have
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})=\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})
. The proof of the first statement follows then the same calculation of the proof of Proposition
4
, with the infinite transition matrix
K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})
. The proof of the remaining statements is trivial by noting that
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 0},({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 1}
given
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
is a HMM. Indeed, Recursion
182
can be interpreted as the forward algorithm for the HMM
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 0},({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})_{t\geq 1}
given
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
.
∎

Proposition 22
.

Under assumptions
7
,
8
, for any
t\geq 1
and
\theta\in\Theta
we have that

\sum_{i}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0

\mathbb{P}-\text{almost surely}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as the proof of Theorem
10
, but we work with the one-individual saturated process.

We can note that because of Assumption
8
if there exists
\theta\in\Theta
,
i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
p_{0}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(i)}=0
and so:

\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w)=p_{0}(w,\theta)=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0,

meaning that
({\mathbf{x}}_{0}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w)
.

Assume now by induction that if there exists
\theta\in\Theta
,
i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
, which is satisfied at time
t=1
because of our previous reasoning.

We can note that:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0&\iff\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta)}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\\
&\iff\forall j\in[M]\quad\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta)}(w_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0,\end{split}

(185)

hence for all
j\in[M]
we either have:

1.

\mathbb{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}=0
which implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
by our inductive assumption, or

2.

K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta)}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
, which implies that there exists
\eta
such that
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
and so, by Assumption
8
,
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
for all
\eta\in[0,C]
and
\theta\in\Theta
meaning
K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta^{\star})}(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
.

from which we conclude:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})\\
&=1-\sum_{x_{t-1}}\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty}=x_{t-1},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}=y_{1:t})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty}=x_{t-1}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})\\
&=1-\sum_{j}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}(w,\theta)}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty})^{(j)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})=1,\end{split}

(186)

hence if
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
then
({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
.

Moving to:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0&\iff\sum_{j}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\\
&\iff\forall j\in[M]\quad\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0,\end{split}

(187)

we then have that for all
j
either:

1.

\mathbb{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}=0
which implies
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
because of what we have proven above, or

2.

G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
which implies
G(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=0
because of Assumption
8
,

meaning that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)\\
&=1-\sum_{x_{t}}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty}=x_{t}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty}=x_{t},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)\\
&=1-\sum_{j}\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)G(w,\theta^{\star})^{(j,i)}=1,\end{split}

(188)

hence if
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
then
({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
.

Consider now:

\begin{split}&\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0\iff\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}\frac{\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}}{\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}}=0\\
&\iff\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0\text{ and }\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0.\end{split}

(189)

Similarly to Theorem
10
we have
\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0
being an almost sure event under
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
, hence:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\text{ and }\sum_{j}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\neq 0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right).\end{split}

(190)

We then just need to work on the event
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0
.

We then have either:

•

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, implying
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
because of what we have proven above, or

•

\sum_{j}G(w,\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}=0
, which tells us that there exists
k\in[M]
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)>0
and
G(w,\theta)^{(i,k)}=0
, note that under
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}=y_{1:t})
we know
k
as we are conditioning on
{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}
;

as:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}=y_{1:t})\\
&\propto\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1,({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(k)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})\\
&=G(w,\theta)^{(i,k)}\mathbb{P}(({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=1|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})=0,\end{split}

(191)

we can then conclude
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}=y_{1:t})
.

With this final result we have shown that our inductive assumption is true at time
t
, hence by induction we can conclude that for any
t\geq 1
:

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
;

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})
;

•

if there exist
\theta\in\Theta,i\in[M]
such that
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}=0
, then
({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}=0
almost surely in
\mathbb{P}(\cdot|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}=y_{1:t})
.

To prove that the random asymptotic CAL is a well-defined algorithm in
\mathbb{P}
we need to prove that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0\right)=1,

(192)

which can be proven by observing that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1,\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0\right)\\
&=\int\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right)\Gamma(dw)\\
&=\int\sum_{y_{1:t-1}}\mathbb{P}\left(\forall\theta\in\Theta,t\geq 1\quad\sum_{i}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(i)}\neq 0|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w,{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1}\right)\\
&\quad\quad\cdot\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{1:t-1}=y_{1:t-1}|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w)\Gamma(dw)\\
&=1.\end{split}

(193)

∎

Proposition 23
.

Under assumptions
6
,
8
,
10
, there exists
{m}_{t}>0
as in Proposition
11
for any
t\geq 1
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Proof.

Consider the following inductive hypothesis. There exists
\bar{m}_{t-1}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall n\in[N],\theta\in\Theta.

As for Proposition
11
, from Assumption
6
we have that
p_{0}(w,\theta)
is continuous in
w,\theta
and both
\mathbb{W}
and
\Theta
are compact, we then get from Weierstrass theorem that there exists a minimum
m_{0}
such that for any realization of
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
and for any
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\right)
:

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}=p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\right)}p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\eqqcolon m_{0},

with
m_{0}>0
as we are considering a minimum over
j\in\textbf{supp}\left(p_{0}(w,\theta)^{(j)}\right)
which excludes all the zeros. As
m_{0}
does not depend on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}
we conclude that the inductive hypothesis holds for
t-1=0
.

We now move to
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)
,
and let
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)
then:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(194)

where the inequality holds with probability
1
under the inductive hypothesis. Several of the remaining inequalities in the proof also hold with probability
1
, although to avoid repetition we do not state this explicitly. Similarly to Proposition
11
:

\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}\geq\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}.

Because of Assumption
8
we have:

K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\Longleftrightarrow K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0\quad\forall\eta\in[0,C],

meaning that

\begin{split}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}&K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}\geq\min_{\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)}.\end{split}

(195)

We can then conclude:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\min_{\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}\\
&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}\min_{\theta\in\Theta,w\in\mathbb{W},\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}(w,\theta)\right)}K_{\eta}(w,\theta)^{(j,i)}.\end{split}

(196)

As
K_{\eta}(w,\theta)
is continuous in
\eta
because of Assumption
10
and also in
w,\theta
because of Assumption
6
, and both
[0,C]
is compact by definition and
\mathbb{W},\Theta
are compact because of Assumption
6
, we can conclude by Weirstrass theorem that there exists a minimum
m_{K}
, hence:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}>0,\end{split}

(197)

where the strictly greater than zero follows from considering the minimum on the support of the transition matrix. As there is no dependence on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}
we conclude that there exist
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Following again the same steps as in Proposition
11
, we have that for an arbitrary realization of
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}
, and
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)
:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\sum_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(198)

where the inequality follows from what we have proven above. Moreover:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\min_{w\in\mathbb{W},\theta\in\Theta}\min_{(i,j)\in\textbf{supp}\left(G(w,\theta)\right)}G(w,\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(199)

and as
G(w,\theta)
is continuous in
w,\theta
because of Assumption
6
and
\mathbb{W},\Theta
are compact because of Assumption
6
we can conclude by Weirstrass theorem that there exists a minimum
m_{G}
:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}>0,\end{split}

(200)

where the strictly greater than zero follows from considering a minimum on the support of the emission matrix. As there is no dependence on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}
we can then conclude that there exist
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K},m_{G}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Finally, consider a realization of
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\infty}
and
i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)
then:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}&\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)},\end{split}

(201)

where the inequality follows from what we have proven above and we know that by definition and by Proposition
22
:

\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\leq 1\text{ and }\sum_{j}G({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i,j)}({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{(j)}\neq 0,

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Following the same steps as in Proposition
11
we can conclude that there exist
\bar{m}_{t-1},m_{K},m_{G}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

We can then set
\bar{m}_{t}\coloneqq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}
and conclude that there exists
\bar{m}_{t}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq\bar{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta,

which closes the induction, meaning that the above statement holds for an arbitrary
t
. As a consequence, we also have that for any
t\geq 1
there exists
{m}_{t}>0
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)^{(i)}\geq{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\mu_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta,

with
{m}_{t}\coloneqq\bar{m}_{t-1}m_{K}m_{G}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Population saturated process.

The population saturated process consists of
\mathbb{O}_{M}
-valued disease states
({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{n,t})_{t\geq 0}
and
\mathbb{O}_{M+1}
-valued observations
({\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{n,t})_{t\geq 1}
, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Given
{\mathbf{w}}_{1},{\mathbf{w}}_{2},\ldots,
(which are the same covariate vectors as in the data-generating process), the individuals and observations
({\mathbf{x}}^{\infty}_{n,t})_{t\geq 0}
,
({\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{n,t})_{t\geq 1}
, are defined to be conditionally independent across
n
, and distributed as follows:

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right),\\
&{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right),\\
&{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}_{n}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\left[({\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]^{\top}\right).\end{split}

(202)

where
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}
is as in Corollary
14
.

Proposition 24
.

Under Assumption
7
,
9
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists constants
e_{t}>0
and
B_{t}>0
such that for any function
f_{t}
with
f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{N})\in[-B_{t},B_{t}]
and
f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{\infty})\in[-B_{t},B_{t}]
almost surely we have:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{t}.\end{split}

(203)

Proof.

Remark that both
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}
and
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{\infty}
are random variables that take values on
\mathbb{O}_{M}
, and similarly
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
and
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}
take values on
\mathbb{O}_{M+1}
. We often write expectations over
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{N}
or
{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{\infty}
(resp.
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
or
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}
) as summations over “
x
” (resp. “
y
”), implicitly it should be understood that we are marginalizing over
x\in\mathbb{O}_{M}
(resp.
y\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}
).

Consider the following inductive hypothesis. There exists
e_{t-1}>0
such that for any function
f_{t-1}
with
f_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,1:{t-1}}^{N})\in[-B_{t-1},B_{t-1}]
and
f_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,1:{t-1}}^{\infty})\in[-B_{t-1},B_{t-1}]
we have:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N})-f_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t-1}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t-1}.\end{split}

(204)

We start by proving it at
t-1=0
, and specifically we want:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})-f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{0}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{0}.\end{split}

(205)

Observe that
f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})-f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})
are conditionally independent given
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
and mean zero because:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]=\sum_{x_{0}}f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},x_{0})p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})=\mathbb{E}\left[f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right].\end{split}

(206)

Moreover, they are almost surely bounded because we are assuming
f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})\in[-B_{0},B_{0}]
and
f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})\in[-B_{0},B_{0}]
almost surely, hence:

\begin{split}\left\lVert f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})-f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2B_{0}.\end{split}

(207)

meaning that by Lemma
5
:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{N})-f_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{0}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(208)

so our inductive hypothesis is true at
t-1=0
for
\bar{e}_{0}=1
.

Consider now a general time
t
, we can rewrite:

\displaystyle f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})

(209)

\displaystyle=f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x

(210)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x

(211)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x

(212)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})

(213)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{t}}^{\infty}),

(214)

meaning that by Minkowski inequality:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(215)

\displaystyle=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(216)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right.\right.

(217)

\displaystyle\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\vvvert_{4}

(218)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right.

(219)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right\vvvert_{4}

(220)

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(221)

Starting from (
216
) we can notice that:

\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{x}}_{0:t-1}^{N}\right]=\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x,

moreover the random variables
f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x
are conditionally independent across
n
given
{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{X}}_{t-1}^{N}
and almost surely bounded by
2B_{t}
, we can then conclude by Lemma
5
:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(222)

Moving to (
218
) we note that by Minkowski inequality:

\begin{split}&\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\left\vvvert\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\vvvert_{4}.\end{split}

(223)

Remark that:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\rvert\\
&\leq B_{t}\sum_{x}\left\lvert({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\rvert\\
&\leq B_{t}\left\lVert K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right\rVert_{\infty},\end{split}

(224)

hence by Assumption
10
we have:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\rvert\\
&\leq B_{t}L\left\lvert\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right\rvert.\end{split}

(225)

We can then rewrite:

\begin{split}&\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\vvvert_{4}\\
