&\leq\frac{B_{t}L}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\Bigg\vvvert\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})-\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(226)

and because of Corollary
14
and Assumption
10
, we can conclude:

\begin{split}&\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}\left[K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right.\right.\\
&\left.\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})\right]x\right\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}LC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\alpha_{t-1}.\end{split}

(227)

Consider (
220
), it is just enough to apply our inductive hypothesis with test function
f_{t-1}
given by:

f_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},x_{0:t-1})=\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},(x_{0:t-1},x))x_{t-1}^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x,

indeed whether we have
x_{0:t-1}={\mathbf{x}}_{n,0:t-1}^{N}
or
x_{0:t-1}={\mathbf{x}}_{n,0:t-1}^{\infty}
in both cases:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},(x_{0:t-1},x))x_{t-1}^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right\rvert\\
&\leq\sum_{x}\left\lvert f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},(x_{0:t-1},x))x_{t-1}^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right\rvert\\
&\leq B_{t}\sum_{x}\left\lvert x_{t-1}^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right\rvert=B_{t}.\end{split}

(228)

Hence:

\begin{split}&\left\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{N},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{N})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right.\\
&\left.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x\right\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t-1}.\end{split}

(229)

The final term to work on is (
221
), but:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})|{\mathbf{x}}_{n,0:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right],\end{split}

(230)

and the random variables:

\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty}),

are defined to be conditionally independent given
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
and bounded by
2B_{t}
. We can then apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{x}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},({\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t-1}}^{\infty},x))({\mathbf{x}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty})^{\top}K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})x-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(231)

By putting everything together we can conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2B_{t}LC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t-1}+2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t-1}+2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[2+(LC+1)\bar{e}_{t-1}\right].\end{split}

(232)

Hence we have proven that our inductive hypothesis is valid at time
t
with the constant
\bar{e}_{t}\coloneqq\left[2+(LMC+1)\bar{e}_{t-1}\right]
, which tells us that for any
t\geq 1
we have:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t},\end{split}

(233)

which also implies:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t},

(234)

as we can rewrite
f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})
as
f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})\mathbb{I}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,0}\in\mathbb{O}_{M})
, where the indicator condition is always satisfied.

We can now move to prove the statement of the proposition, with
f_{t}
as therein:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{t}.\end{split}

(235)

Observe that:

\displaystyle f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})

(236)

\displaystyle=f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}

(237)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\left[\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\right]

(238)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{t}}^{\infty}),

(239)

then by Minkowski inequality:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(240)

\displaystyle=\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(241)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\left[\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(242)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(243)

Starting from (
241
) we can notice that:

\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{X}}_{1:t}^{N}\right]=\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s},

moreover the random variables:

f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s},

are conditionally independent given
{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{X}}_{1:t}^{N}
and bounded by
2B_{t}
, hence we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Consider now (
242
) and notice that if we consider the test function:

h_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},x_{0:t})=\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}(x_{s})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s},

whether we have
x_{0:t}={\mathbf{x}}_{n,0:t}^{N}
or
x_{0:t}={\mathbf{x}}_{n,0:t}^{\infty}
in both cases:

\left\lvert\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}(x_{s})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\right\rvert\leq B_{t}\sum_{y_{1:t}}\left\lvert\prod_{s=1}^{t}(x_{s})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\right\rvert=B_{t}.

The term (
242
) is then an application of (
234
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\left[\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{N})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t}.\end{split}

(244)

The last term we need to work on is (
243
), but:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})|{\mathbf{W}}^{N},{\mathbf{x}}_{n,{0:t}}^{\infty}\right]|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right\}\\
&=\sum_{y_{1:t}}\mathbb{E}\left[f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}|{\mathbf{W}}^{N}\right],\end{split}

(245)

and the random variables:

\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty}),

are defined to be conditionally independent given
{\mathbf{W}}^{N}
and bounded by
2B_{t}
, hence we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\sum_{y_{1:t}}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},y_{1:t})\prod_{s=1}^{t}({\mathbf{x}}_{n,s}^{\infty})^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta^{\star})y_{s}-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

By putting everything together we get:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{N})-f_{t}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,{1:t}}^{\infty})\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{e}_{t}+2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=2B_{t}\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(2+\bar{e}_{t}),\end{split}

(246)

which conclude the proof under
e_{t}\coloneqq(2+\bar{e}_{t})
.
∎

Saturated CAL algorithm.

We refer to the following recursion as the “saturated CAL algorithm”.

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta),

(247)

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right\},

where
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
132
).

Proposition 25
.

Under assumptions
7
,
8
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N},t\geq 1,n\in[N]
and
\theta\in\Theta
we have that
\sum_{i}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{(i)}\neq 0

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as Theorem
10
, where we can replicate the same of Proposition
6
, Proposition
7
, Proposition
8
, and Proposition
9
for the saturated CAL. The only difference can be found in the prediction step where instead of

K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{N}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
we have
K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
which similarly implies that there exists
\eta=\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
such that
K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}=0
, which allows us to follow the same argument as in Proposition
7
.
∎

Proposition 26
.

Under assumptions
6
,
8
,
10
, for any
t\geq 1
there exists
{m}_{t}>0
as in Proposition
11
for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\in[N]
such that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}\geq{m}_{t}\quad\forall i\in\textbf{supp}\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right)=1\quad\forall\theta\in\Theta.

Proof.

The proof follows the same steps as the proof of Proposition
11
, indeed the only difference between the CAL and the saturated CAL is the use of the saturated dynamic from which we still get:

\begin{split}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}&K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\infty}_{t-1}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)}\geq\min_{\eta\in[0,C]}\min_{j\in\textbf{supp}\left(K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(\cdot,i)}\right)}K_{\eta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(j,i)},\end{split}

(248)

as in the proof of Proposition
11
.
∎

To conclude the section we establish that the incremental terms in the logarithm of the CAL approximated the corresponding quantities from the saturated CAL algorithm.

Proposition 27
.

Under assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
, there exists
\chi_{t}>0
such that for any
t\geq 1
,
n\in[N]
:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{t}.\end{split}

(249)

Proof.

Using Proposition
11
and Proposition
26
for the lower bound, together with the fact that
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
is a one hot encoding vector and the
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\cdot}^{\cdot}
are probability vectors for the upper bound, we can conclude that both
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
and
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
are such that:

\displaystyle 0<{m}_{t}\leq({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\leq 1,\quad 0<{m}_{t}\leq({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\leq 1,

(250)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

As the the function
u\mapsto\log(u)
is Lipschitz on the compact interval
[{m}_{t},1]
we have:

\begin{split}&\left\lvert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right\rvert\\
&\leq\frac{1}{{m}_{t}}\left\lvert({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\rvert,\end{split}

(251)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. From the above we can conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{{m}_{t}}\Bigg\vvvert({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(252)

meaning that it remains to bound:

\Bigg\vvvert({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

Consider as an inductive hypothesis that there exists a constant
\bar{\chi}_{t-1}>0
such that for any random vector
{\mathbf{f}}_{n}
which satisfies
\left\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t-1}.\end{split}

(253)

Observe that this is valid at
t-1=0
since
{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)=\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
, therefore we have:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}=0.\end{split}

(254)

In order to show that the inductive hypothesis holds at time
t
, let us first consider the prediction step, and observe that:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(255)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(256)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(257)

Starting from (
256
), we have:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}-{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\left\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}\Bigg\vvvert\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2BC\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\gamma_{t-1}+1),\end{split}

(258)

which follows in the same way as the proof of Proposition
17
, see (
146
).

Moving to (
257
), we can apply our inductive hypothesis on the random vector:
K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}
since
\left\lVert K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, hence:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t-1}.\end{split}

(259)

By putting everything together we can conclude:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

\displaystyle\leq 2B[C(\gamma_{t-1}+1)+\bar{\chi}_{t-1}]\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}

(260)

\displaystyle\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1},

(261)

where
\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}\coloneqq C(\gamma_{t-1}+1)+\bar{\chi}_{t-1}
. Given the above we also have:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\Bigg\vvvert\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}-\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1},\end{split}

(262)

which is just an application of the previous result on the bounded random variable
G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{f}}_{n}
, as we know
\left\lVert G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)f\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
is bounded
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Now we need to work on:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(263)

Note that by using
G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)
for the matrix with elements
G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{(i,j)}=\frac{G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{(i,j)}}{\boldsymbol{\mu}^{(j)}}
where
\frac{0}{0}=0
by convention, we can rewrite everything in a more compact way:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.\end{split}

(264)

By Minkowski inequality we can then conclude:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(265)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]

(266)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(267)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]

(268)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(269)

Starting from (
267
), we remark that:

x^{\top}G_{\mu}b-x^{\top}G_{\widetilde{\mu}}b=\sum_{i,j}x^{(i)}y^{(j)}\frac{G^{(i,j)}\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}-G^{(i,j)}\mu^{(j)}}{\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}{\mu}^{(j)}}=\sum_{i,j}x^{(i)}\frac{y^{(j)}}{\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}{\mu}^{(j)}}G^{(i,j)}\left(\widetilde{\mu}^{(j)}-\mu^{(j)}\right).

(270)

Hence we can reformulate (
267
):

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\left\{\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}^{\top}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\\
&\qquad\qquad\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4},\end{split}

(271)

from which we can notice that for any
{\mathbf{w}}_{n}
:

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\left\{\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\left\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left\lVert{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\rVert_{\infty},\end{split}

(272)

where the first step follows from
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)=\left[{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}},\theta)\right]^{\top}
and the elementwise ratio
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
is well-defined because of Proposition
25
.

As from Proposition
26
we know that the saturated CAL is almost surely bounded we have:

\begin{split}&\left\lVert\left\{\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\frac{\left\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}}{{m}_{t}}\leq\frac{B}{{m}_{t}},\end{split}

(273)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Hence we can apply (
262
) as we are considering an almost surely bounded random vector:

\left\{\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]

and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\left\{\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right\}^{\top}\odot\left[{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\oslash\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\\
&\qquad\qquad\left[\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\chi_{t}}{{m}_{t}}.\end{split}

(274)

Moving to (
269
), we can observe that:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\\
&\qquad-\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top}\left[G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&=\Bigg\vvvert\left[{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right]^{\top}\left[{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}},\end{split}

(275)

where we can apply (
262
) to the test vector
{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\odot G_{\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
, as it is almost surely bounded.

By putting everything together we can conclude:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\chi_{t}}{{m}_{t}}+2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}},

(276)

which closes our inductive hypothesis by setting
{\bar{\chi}_{t}}=\frac{\chi_{t}}{{m}_{t}}+{\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}}
, and so we can conclude that for any
t\geq 1
there exists
\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1},\bar{\chi}_{t}
such that for any test random vector
{\mathbf{f}}_{n}
, with
\left\lVert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely:

•

\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}
;

•

\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}
;

•

\Bigg\vvvert{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-{\mathbf{f}}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2B\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t}
.

As
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
is almost surely bounded
\left\lVert{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 1
we can conclude:

\Bigg\vvvert({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1},

so we can conclude our proof by setting
\chi_{t}\coloneqq\frac{\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}
as:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{{m}_{t}}\Bigg\vvvert({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)-({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\bar{\chi}_{t|t-1}}{{m}_{t}}.\end{split}

(277)

∎

C.5
Strong consistency

Contrast function.

In Proposition
28
below we establish the convergence of the rescaled logarithm of the CAL,
\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}
in the large population limit, and in Theorem
29
show how
\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}
converges to a contrast function.

Proposition 28
.

Under assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
and for any
\theta\in\Theta
let:

\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)\coloneqq\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right),

then
N^{-1}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]
as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. Moreover:

\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right].

(278)

Proof.

Consider the definition of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
from (
23
), the definition of
\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
from (
247
), the definition of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)
from (
182
), and define:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,0}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta),

(279)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\left[\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\coloneqq\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}\right\},

where
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
132
) and
{\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{n,t}
is defined in (
202
).

We can then consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]

(280)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}(\log\left({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)

(281)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)

(282)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right],

(283)

by Minkowski inequality we conclude:

\displaystyle\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(284)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(285)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}

(286)

\displaystyle+\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}.

(287)

Starting from (
285
), we can apply Proposition
27
to obtain:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{t}.\end{split}

(288)

Consider now (
286
), it is important to observe that when we compare (
247
) with (
279
), the only difference between the two is that (
247
) uses
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}
while (
279
) uses the population saturated process
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}
. Hence, if we look at
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
as a function of
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{N}
and
({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
as a function of
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,1:t}^{\infty}
we are considering the same function evaluated in different arguments; we can define for a fixed
\theta
the function
h_{t}^{\theta}(w,y_{1:t})
which is such that
h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})=({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
and
h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})=({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
.
Because of Proposition
26
, for all
i\in[M]
we have that
\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}>m_{t}
, meaning that
h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})\in[m_{t},1]
almost surely. Similarly, because of Proposition
23
, for all
i\in[M]
we have that
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)^{(i)}>m_{t}
, meaning that
h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})\in[m_{t},1]
almost surely. This follows as (
202
) consists of repeating (
181
)
N
times. We can then consider
\log(h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}))\in[\log(m_{t}),0]
and
\log(h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}))\in[\log(m_{t}),0]
almost surely and apply Proposition
24
as both
\log(h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N}))
and
\log(h_{t}^{\theta}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty}))
are almost surely in
[\log(m_{t}),\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert]
:

\begin{split}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{t}.\end{split}

(289)

For (
287
), note that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right],

(290)

see the definitions of
{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty},{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty},\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)
in Section
C.4
and the definition of
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)
in (
279
) at the beginning of the proof. Moreover,

\left\lVert\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\rVert\leq 2\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert,

(291)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, because of the previous reasoning. As we are considering averages of random variables that are mean zero, bounded, and independent, we can apply Lemma
5
and conclude:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert.\end{split}

(292)

By putting everything together we obtain:

\begin{split}&\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}\\
&\leq 2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{t}+2\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}e_{t}+2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert\\
&=2\sqrt[4]{6}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left[\chi_{t}+\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert(e_{t}+1)\right].\end{split}

(293)

By applying Markov’s inequality, for any
\iota>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\rvert>\iota\right)\\
&=\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\rvert^{4}>\iota^{4}\right)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\iota^{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\rvert^{4}\right]\\
&=\iota^{-4}\Bigg\vvvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\Bigg\vvvert_{4}^{4}\\
&\leq 6\frac{2^{4}}{\iota^{4}}N^{-2}\left[\chi_{t}+\left\lvert\log(m_{t})\right\rvert(e_{t}+1)\right]^{4},\end{split}

(294)

where the last bound follows from (
293
). We then conclude that:

\sum_{N=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left\lvert\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\rvert>\iota\right)<\infty,

(295)

hence by the Borel-Cantelli lemma:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\to 0,

(296)

as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, or equivalently
\frac{1}{N}\ell^{N}_{t}(\theta)
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]
as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

For the final statement of the proposition it is enough to observe that as
{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}
is a one-hot encoding vector:

\log\left(({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)=({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)

under the convention
0\log 0=0
, and from Proposition
21
we have:

{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right).

Hence, by the tower rule:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\right]\right\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty})^{\top}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\right]\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right\}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right].\end{split}

(297)

∎

Because of Proposition
28
, we can conclude that the CAL has a contrast function which is an expected Kullback-Leibler divergence, as in the following theorem. Recall from (
182
) the definition
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)
.

Theorem 29
.

Under assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
, for any
T\geq 1
and
\theta\in\Theta
let:

\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)\coloneqq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\ell_{t}^{N}(\theta)

then:

\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star}),

as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, where:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\}.

(298)

Moreover:

\theta^{\star}\in\Theta^{\star}\coloneqq\argmax_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star}).

(299)

Proof.

Because of Proposition
28
:

\begin{split}&\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}\to\sum_{t=1}^{T}\int\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}\right)\right|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right]\Gamma(dw),\end{split}

(300)

as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, where we notice that:

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}\right)\right|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right]\\
&=\mathbb{E}\left\{\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)}{\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})}\right)\right|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right]\right|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right\}\\
&=-\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right\},\end{split}

(301)

hence:

\begin{split}&\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}\\
&\to-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\int\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right\}\Gamma(dw),\end{split}

(302)

as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely, from which we conclude the first part of the proof as:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})=-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\int\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right\}\Gamma(dw).

(303)

The KL-divergence is always greater than or equal to zero, and equal to zero if and only if the two distributions are equal. Hence the maximal value of the negative KL-divergence is zero, and we have:

\theta^{\star}\in\Theta^{\star}=\argmax_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star}),

(304)

which concludes the proof.
∎

Uniform almost sure convergence.

Theorem
29
proves the convergence
\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})

pointwise
in
\theta
. In order to make statements about the convergence of the maximizer of
\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)
in the same vein as
Whitehouse et al. (2023)
, we must show this convergence is uniform. To proceed we will use the following results.

Definition 30
.

Let
(\mathcal{H}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
be a sequence of random functions
\mathcal{H}_{N}:\theta\in\Theta\mapsto\mathcal{H}_{N}(\theta)\in\mathbb{R}
where
\Theta
is a metric space. We say that
(\mathcal{H}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
are stochastically equicontinuous if there exists an event
E
of probability
1
, such that for all
\iota>0
and
\omega\in E
, there exists
N(\omega)
and
\delta>0
such that
N>N(\omega)
implies:

\sup_{|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|<\delta}|\mathcal{H}_{N}(\omega,\theta_{1})-\mathcal{H}_{N}(\omega,\theta_{2})|<\iota.

Lemma 31
.

Assume
\Theta
is a compact metric space and let
(\mathcal{H}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
be a sequence of random functions
\mathcal{H}_{N}:\theta\in\Theta\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(\theta)\in\mathbb{R}
. If there exists a continuous function
\mathcal{H}
such that for all
\theta\in\Theta
we have
|\mathcal{H}_{N}(\theta)-\mathcal{H}(\theta)|\overset{a.s.}{\rightarrow}0
, and
(\mathcal{H}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
are stochastically equicontinuous, then:

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\mathcal{H}_{N}(\theta)-\mathcal{H}(\theta)|\overset{a.s.}{\rightarrow}0.

That is
\mathcal{H}_{N}(\theta)
converges to
\mathcal{H}(\theta)
almost surely as
N\rightarrow\infty
, uniformly in
\theta
.

Proof.

See
Andrews (1992)
.
∎

Lemma 32
.

Under Assumptions
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
,

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta)}{N}-\frac{\ell_{1:T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})}{N}-\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})\right|\to 0,

(305)

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

By Theorem
29
we have pointwise convergence. Hence, by Lemma
31
it is enough to show that

\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta)\coloneqq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n\in[N]}({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{N})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}^{N}({\mathbf{w}}_{n},\theta)\right),

(306)

is stochastically equicontinuous. Define

\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta)\coloneqq\int\mathbb{E}\left[\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})^{\top}\log\left(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}={\mathbf{w}}\right]\Gamma(d{\mathbf{w}}).

(307)

Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta
and consider the decomposition

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta_{1})-\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta_{2})|\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta_{1})-\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta_{1})|

(308)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta_{2})-\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta_{2})|

(309)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta_{1})-\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta_{2})|.

(310)

Note that all of these quantities are well defined by Theorem
10
and by the fact that
\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\infty}_{t}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\neq 0
because of Proposition
22
.

Let
E\subset\Omega
such that
\mathbb{P}(E)=1
. Let
\theta,\theta^{\star}\in\Theta
,
\omega\in E
, and
\iota>0
. By Theorem
29
we have almost sure convergence of (
308
) and (
309
) to
0
, hence there exists an
{N}(\omega)
such that for all
N>{N}(\omega)
these terms are bounded by
\iota/3
.

It remains to show that there exists a
\delta
such that for
|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|<\delta
implies that (
310
) is bounded by
\iota/3
. This follows directly from noticing that
\mathcal{C}_{t}^{\infty}(\theta)
comprises a composition of continuous functions of our model quantities
p_{0},K,
and
G
, which are themselves continuous functions of
\theta
because of Assumption
6
.
∎

Theorem 33
.

Let
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
10
hold and let
\hat{{\theta}}_{N}
be a maximizer of
\ell_{1:T}^{N}({\theta})
. Then
\hat{{\theta}}_{N}
converges to
\Theta^{\star}
as
N\to\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The proof follows in the same manner as that of Theorem 1 in
Whitehouse et al. (2023)
, we include it for completeness.

Let
\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta)
be as defined by (
298
) and let
\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\theta)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{C}_{t}^{N}(\theta)
be as in Equation (
306
). We have that
\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\hat{\theta}_{N})\geq\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}({\theta})
for all
\theta\in\Theta^{\star}
. Furthermore
{\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta)\geq 0}
for all
\theta\in\Theta
. We can combine these inequalities to obtain:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\hat{\theta}_{n})

(311)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})+\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\theta^{\star})-\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\hat{\theta}_{n})+\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\hat{\theta}_{n})-\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\hat{\theta}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left|\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta)-\mathcal{C}_{T}^{N}(\theta)\right|\rightarrow 0\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely,}

by Lemma
32
. Hence
\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\hat{\theta}_{n})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},{\theta^{\star}})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Now assume for purposes of contradiction that there is some positive probability that
\hat{\theta}_{n}
does not converge to the set
\Theta^{\star}
, i.e. assume that there is an event
E\subset\Omega
with
\mathbb{P}(E)>0
such that for all
\omega\in E
there exists a
\delta>0
such that for infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have
{\hat{\theta}_{n}(\omega)}
is not in the open neighborhood
B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})=\{\theta\in\Theta:\exists\theta^{\prime}\in\Theta^{\star}:\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|<\delta\}
.
Since
\Theta
is compact, the set
B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})^{c}=\Theta\setminus B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})
is closed, bounded, and therefore compact. Furthermore,
\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta)
is continuous in
\theta
. By the extreme value theorem this means that there exists a
\theta^{\prime}\in B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})^{c}
such that for all
\theta\in B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})^{c}
:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta)\leq\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\prime})

Furthermore, since
\theta^{\prime}\notin\Theta^{\star}
there exists
\iota>0
such that:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\prime})<\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\iota.

By our assumption we have for each
\omega\in E
there are infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\hat{\theta}_{n}(\omega)\in B_{\delta}(\Theta^{\star})^{c}
. But this implies that for each
\omega\in E
there are infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\hat{\theta}_{n}(\omega))\leq\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\prime})<\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\iota,

\implies|\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\theta^{\star})-\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta^{\star},\hat{\theta}_{n}(\omega))|>\iota,

which contradicts (
311
). Hence we must have that
\hat{\theta}_{n}
converges to the set
\Theta^{\star}

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.
∎

C.5.1
Identifiability

Definition 34
.

Let
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}\}_{t\geq 1}
be generated according to the process defined by equations (
181
)
with data-generating parameter
\theta^{\star}\in\Theta
. Denote the law of
\{{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}\}_{t\geq 1}
conditional on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w
with
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
.

Lemma 35
.

Let
\theta^{\star}\in\Theta
.
For any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta^{\star}\coloneqq\argmax_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})
we have that
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{1},w}=\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{2},w}
for
\Gamma
-almost all
w\in\mathbb{W}
.

Proof.

Recall from (
298
) the definition of the contrast function:

\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})\coloneqq-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\}.

(312)

where
\theta^{\star}
is the DGP and
\theta
is a candidate parameter. With
\theta^{\star}
fixed, we want to characterize the set of maximizers of
\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})
in the first argument, i.e. the set
\Theta^{\star}\coloneqq\argmax_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})
.
Let
w\in\mathbb{W}
and consider the conditional expectation

\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\},

(313)

this is the expectation of a KL-divergence between categorical distributions parameterized by the random vectors
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)
. Recall that according to the recursive definition of these vectors given in (
182
), conditional on
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w
these vectors are random solely as functions of
{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
, with no other sources of stochasticity. Hence this conditional expectation is a summation over
y_{1:t-1}\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}^{t-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}=w\right]\right\}

(314)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y_{1:t-1}}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}({\mathbf{y}}^{\infty}_{1:t-1}=y_{1:t-1})\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\right)\right]

(315)

where the unbolded
\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
and
\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)
(similarly to the process defined by Equation (
181
)) are calculated with the recursions:

\displaystyle\pi_{0}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq p_{0}(w,\theta),

(316)

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)=\int d(w,\widetilde{w},\theta)^{\top}\bar{\pi}_{t-1}^{\infty}(\widetilde{w},\theta)\Gamma(d\widetilde{w}),

\displaystyle\pi_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\pi_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}K_{\bar{\eta}_{t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)}(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\left[\pi_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}G(w,\theta)\right]^{\top},

\displaystyle\pi_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\coloneqq\pi_{t|t-1}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\odot\left\{\left[G(w,\theta)\oslash\left(1_{M}\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)^{\top}\right)\right]y_{t}\right\},

where the dependence of various quantities on
y_{1:t}
is not shown in the notation.

By properties of the KL-divergence we have that

\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\right)\right]=0\iff\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)=\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star}),

(317)

and

\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\right)\right]>0\iff\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)\neq\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star}),

(318)

which makes it clear that
\theta^{\star}\in\Theta^{\star}
, as already mentioned in (
299
). It then follows from Equation (
314
) that if
\theta\in\Theta^{\star}
then for

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
-almost all paths
{y}_{1:t-1}\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}^{t-1}
we must have
\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)=\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
. Now, considering the full contrast function we have by the tower rule:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{T}(\theta,\theta^{\star})

\displaystyle\coloneqq-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]\right\}

(319)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbf{KL}\left[\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)||\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta)\right)\right]|{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\right\}\right\}.

(320)

Here we are summing over
t\in[T]
and taking the expectation of (
314
) over
{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\Gamma
. It therefore follows that if
\theta\in\Theta^{\star}
then for all
t\in[T]
,
\Gamma-
almost all
w\in\mathbb{W}
, and
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
-almost all paths
{y}_{1:t-1}\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}^{t-1}
, we must have that
\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)=\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
.

To complete the proof, recall that by Proposition
21
if
{\mathbf{y}}_{1:T}\sim\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
we have the conditional distributions
{\mathbf{y}}_{t}^{\infty}|{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty},{\mathbf{w}}^{\infty}\sim\mbox{Cat}\left(\cdot|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{t}^{\infty}({\mathbf{w}}^{\infty},\theta^{\star})\right)
. Hence for
\Gamma-
almost all
w\in\mathbb{W}
,
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta^{\star},w}
-almost all paths
{y}_{1:T}\in\mathbb{O}_{M+1}^{T}
and any
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\Theta^{\star}
we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{1},w}({{\mathbf{y}}}_{1:T}^{\infty}=y_{1:T})&=\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{1},w}({{\mathbf{y}}}_{t}^{\infty}=y_{t}|{{\mathbf{y}}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})\\
&=\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left(y_{t}|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta_{1})\right)\\
&=\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left(y_{t}|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})\right)\\
&=\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mbox{Cat}\left(y_{t}|\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta_{2})\right)\\
&=\prod_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{2},w}({{\mathbf{y}}}_{t}^{\infty}=y_{t}|{{\mathbf{y}}}_{1:t-1}^{\infty}=y_{1:t-1})\\
&=\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{2},w}({{\mathbf{y}}}_{1:T}^{\infty}=y_{1:T}),\end{split}

(321)

where we used that
\theta\in\Theta^{\star}
implies
\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta)=\mu_{t}^{\infty}(w,\theta^{\star})
. Assumption
8
ensures that the support of
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta,w}
does not depend on
\theta
, and hence
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{1},w}=\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\theta_{2},w}
for
\Gamma-
almost all
w\in\mathbb{W}
.
∎

Appendix D
Experiments

D.1
Computational considerations

Algorithm
1
requires only
N
repetitions of simple linear algebra operations on
M
-dimensional vectors and
M\times M
matrices at each time step. The resulting computational cost is
\mathcal{O}(TNM^{2}+TN\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N))
, where
T
and
N
arise from recursive operations over time and individuals,
M^{2}
accounts for vector-matrix operations, and
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)
is the cost of evaluating the
\eta
function for a fixed population size
N
.

Table 4:
Memory and running time summary.
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)
is the model specific cost of evaluating
\eta
for a fixed population size
N
. For the SMC,
P
is the number of particles, while
\mathcal{C}_{q}(M,1)
is the cost of computing the parameters of the categorical distribution
q
which is used as a proposal (see
Rimella et al. (2023)
for an example). The memory requirement assumes that everything is stored over time steps.

Memory

Running time

Data simulation

\mathcal{O}(TN)

\mathcal{O}(TN\mathcal{C}_{Cat}(M,1)+TN\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N))

SMC

\mathcal{O}(TNP)

\mathcal{O}(TN\mathcal{C}_{Cat}(M,P)+TNP\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)+TN\mathcal{C}_{q}(M,P))

CAL

\mathcal{O}(TNM)

\mathcal{O}(TNM^{2}+TN\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N))

The cost
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)
is model-specific. For instance, for the motivating example in sections
1.1
and
2.3
, we have a cost of
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)=\mathcal{O}(N)
for the homogeneous-mixing case and a cost of
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)=\mathcal{O}(N^{2})
for the heterogeneous-mixing scenario or other dense spatial interactions
(
Jewell et al., 2009
;
Rimella and others, 2023
)
. Remark that the cost
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N)
is inherent to the model, rather than a feature of the CAL, as even simulating data from the model would incur in a cost of the same order:
\mathcal{O}(TN\mathcal{C}_{Cat}(M,1)+TN\mathcal{C}_{\eta}(N))
, where
\mathcal{C}_{Cat}(M,1)
is the cost of simulating a single draw from a categorical distribution with
M
categories. Algorithm
1
requires computing probability vectors
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t|t-1}
,
\boldsymbol{\mu}_{n,t}
, and
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}
for each individual
n
at every time step. If all these vectors are stored over
T
time steps, the memory requirement is
\mathcal{O}(TNM)
. The memory requirement of storing the data is
\mathcal{O}(TN)
. The resulting computational cost is significantly cheaper than SMC, which would require many simulations of each individual. Table
4
provides an SMC-CAL complexity comparison summary. Remark that if the user is only interested in the likelihood computation, it is possible to reduce the memory consumption of the SMC to
\mathcal{O}(NP)
, and similarly to
\mathcal{O}(NM)
for the CAL.

D.2
Hamiltonian Monte Carlo for homogeneous-mixing SIS

We use the parameter settings
p_{0}=0.01,\beta=0.2,{\mathbf{b}}_{I}=0.5,{\mathbf{b}}_{S}=1.0,\gamma=0.1,{\mathbf{b}}_{R}=-0.5,q_{S}=0.2,q_{I}=0.5,q_{Se}=0.9,q_{Sp}=0.95
. We treat
p_{0},q_{Se},q_{Sp}
as known. To create a warm start for our HMC sampler we consider
100
random initializations, where each parameter is drawn from a standard Gaussian, and perform
1000
steps of Adam optimization with a learning rate of
0.1
. Across the
100
initializations, we choose the resulting parameter values with the highest CAL log-likelihood as a warm-start for our HMC sampler which we then run for
200000
iterations. We use uninformative independent Gaussian priors each with mean
0
and standard deviation
10
.

The learning rate is adapted every
1000
iteration to fall within the acceptance range
[0.55,0.75]
. Specifically, if the acceptance range is lower than
0.55
then for the next
1000
iterations it is decreased by
35\%
, while if it is higher than
0.75
it is increased by
35\%
, otherwise if it falls within the range is kept stable. We run the 201000 HMC iterations and then we choose only the last iterations in which the acceptance rate was stable. We get acceptance rates within the range
0.60-0.70
, which are close to the optimal acceptance rate of
0.65

(
Neal, 2012
)
. We further apply a thinning procedure that select
10000
samples equally spaced in time (number of iterations).

D.3
Gradient-based calibration for heterogeneously-mixing SIS models

Model 1

Here we provide all the details on Model 1. As initial infection probabilities, we consider:

p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}1-p_{0}\\
p_{0}\end{bmatrix},

(322)

where
p_{0}\in[0,1]
. We consider an interaction term:

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}{\exp\left\{-\frac{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{n}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\|^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1},

(323)

where
\phi>0
and
{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\in\mathbb{R}
as we are considering a single covariate. The
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}
is then used in the transition matrix:

K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}}({\mathbf{w}}_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}\exp{\left(-h\beta\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{S}\}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}-h\epsilon\right)}&1-\exp{\left(-h\beta\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{S}\}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}-h\epsilon\right)}\\
1-\exp{\left(-h\gamma_{n}\right)}&\exp{\left(-h\gamma_{n}\right)}\end{bmatrix},

(324)

with
\log\gamma_{n}=\log\gamma+{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{R}
and where
\beta,\gamma>0
and
{\mathbf{b}}_{S},{\mathbf{b}}_{R}\in\mathbb{R}
.

The observation model is given by the matrix:

G({\mathbf{w}}_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}1-q_{S}&q_{S}q_{Sp}&q_{S}(1-q_{Sp})\\
1-q_{I}&q_{I}(1-q_{Se})&q_{I}q_{Se}\end{bmatrix},

where
q_{S},q_{Se},q_{I},q_{Sp}\in[0,1]
.

Figure
7
provides a graphical representation of the optimization of Model 1.

Figure 7:
CAL parameters values during optimization for Model 1 from Section
5.2
over the gradient steps of Adam and across different population sizes (from top to bottom). Lines refer to the best out of
10
optimizations on different datasets. Blue and black colors are used in the same plot to distinguish across parameter components.

Model 2

Here we provide all the details on Model 2. Everything is as in Model 1 but the spatial location is now set to the centroid of the community the individual is in. As a consequence, there is also a computationally cheaper representation of
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}
, which exploits the fact that some individuals belong to the same community. Denote with
{{\mathbf{m}}}^{c}_{i}
the centroid of community
i
and with
{\mathbf{a}}_{i}
the set of individuals within the community, i.e.
n\in{\mathbf{a}}_{i}
if
n
is in the community
i
. We can then observe that for
n\in{{\mathbf{a}}}_{j}
:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}{\exp\left\{-\frac{\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in{{\mathbf{a}}}_{i}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}{\exp\left\{-\frac{\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in{{\mathbf{a}}}_{i}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}{\exp\left\{-\frac{\|{{\mathbf{m}}}^{c}_{j}-{{\mathbf{m}}}^{c}_{i}\|^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1},\end{split}

(325)

from which we observe that for any
n,k\in{{\mathbf{a}}}_{j}
we have
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}=\boldsymbol{\eta}_{k,t-1}
, hence we just need to compute the interaction term for each community. This reduces the computational cost of computing all
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}
from
N^{2}
to
N
times the number of communities.

Figure
8
provides a graphical representation of the optimization of Model 2.

Figure 8:
CAL parameters values during optimization for Model 2 from Section
5.2
over the gradient steps of Adam and across different population sizes (from top to bottom). Lines refer to the best out of
10
optimizations on different datasets. Blue and black colors are used in the same plot to distinguish across parameter components.

D.4
Calibration and filtering for heterogeneously-mixing SIR

Well-specified model

Here we provide all the details on the correctly specified model from Section
5.3
. As initial infection probabilities, we consider:

p_{0}({\mathbf{w}}_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}1-p_{0}\mathbb{I}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{n}\in\left[-\infty,5\right]\times\left[8,+\infty\right]\right)\\
p_{0}\mathbb{I}\left({\mathbf{z}}_{n}\in\left[-\infty,5\right]\times\left[8,+\infty\right]\right)\\
0\end{bmatrix},

(326)

where
p_{0}\in[0,1]
. We consider an interaction term:

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{\exp\left\{-\frac{\|{\mathbf{z}}_{n}-{\mathbf{z}}_{k}\|^{2}}{2\phi}\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi}}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1},

(327)

where
\phi>0
and
{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\in\mathbb{R}
as we are considering a single covariate. The
\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}
is then used in the transition matrix:

\begin{split}&K_{\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}}({\mathbf{w}}_{n})\\
&=\begin{bmatrix}\exp{\left(-h\beta\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{S}\}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}-h\epsilon\right)}&1-\exp{\left(-h\beta\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{S}\}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}-h\epsilon\right)}&0\\
0&\exp{\left(-h\gamma_{n}\right)}&1-\exp{\left(-h\gamma_{n}\right)}\\
0&0&1\end{bmatrix},\end{split}

(328)

with
\log\gamma_{n}=\log\gamma+{\mathbf{c}}_{n}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{R}
and where
\beta,\gamma>0
and
{\mathbf{b}}_{S},{\mathbf{b}}_{R}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Half of the population is forced to be unobserved and misreporting is not allowed. We call
U
the set of individuals that are always unobserved, the observation model is then given by:

G({\mathbf{w}}_{n})=\begin{bmatrix}1-q_{S}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)&q_{S}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)&0&0\\
1-q_{I}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)&0&q_{I}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)&0\\
1-q_{R}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)&0&0&q_{R}\mathbb{I}(n\notin U)\end{bmatrix},

where
q_{S},q_{I},q_{R}\in[0,1]
.

Misspecified model

The initial infection probabilities, the transition matrix, and the observation model are the same as for the well-specified model. However, the interaction term is:

\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\neq 0)+\bar{{\mathbf{z}}}_{n}\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|=0))^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}.

(329)

as explained in the main paper. We can notice that the big advantage of this formulation, as for Model 2, is the computational cost indeed:

\begin{split}\boldsymbol{\eta}_{n,t-1}&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k\in[N]}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\neq 0)+\bar{{\mathbf{z}}}_{n}\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|=0))^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in{\mathbf{a}}_{i}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|\neq 0)+\bar{{\mathbf{z}}}_{n}\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{n}-{\mathbf{m}}_{k}\|=0))^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i}\sum_{k\in{\mathbf{a}}_{i}}\begin{bmatrix}0\\
\exp\{{\mathbf{c}}_{k}^{\top}{\mathbf{b}}_{I}\}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\phi^{2}}}\exp\left\{-\frac{(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{j}^{c}-{\mathbf{m}}_{i}^{c}\|\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{j}^{c}-{\mathbf{m}}_{i}^{c}\|\neq 0)+\bar{{\mathbf{z}}}_{j}^{c}\mathbb{I}(\|{\mathbf{m}}_{j}^{c}-{\mathbf{m}}_{i}^{c}\|=0))^{2}}{2\phi^{2}}\right\}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}{\mathbf{x}}_{k,t-1}\\
\end{split}

(330)

where
{\mathbf{m}}_{i}^{c}
is the centroid of community
i
,
\bar{{\mathbf{z}}}_{i}^{c}
is the mean distance within community
i
,
{\mathbf{a}}_{i}
is the set of individuals within community
i
, and we assume
n\in{\mathbf{a}}_{j}
.

The considered metrics for comparison are:

•

cross-entropy loss:

-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n\in[N]}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}^{\top}\log(\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t});

•

accuracy:

\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{n\in[N]}\mathbb{I}\left(\argmax_{i}{\mathbf{x}}_{n,t}=\argmax_{i}\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}\right)\right]\cdot 100\%.

We now define the baseline classifiers. To build a classifier we need to create a vector of probabilities, which represents the probability of estimating the different states, e.g. for the CAL this is
\pi_{n,t}
. Let us start with the “Random” classifier, here our vector of probabilities for estimating is:
\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{g}=\mathbb{I}\left({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(M+1)}=1\right)(1_{3}\oslash 3)+\mathbb{I}\left({\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(M+1)}=0\right){\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}
, meaning that we estimate at random if the individual is unreported, otherwise we estimate with what is reported. “Prev. uncertain” and “Prev. certain” are more complicated and we define them via Algorithm
2
. Here, if the individual is reported, we estimate the individual’s state with what is reported, otherwise, we have a confidence parameter
g
which tells us how confident we are with predicting the
n
th individual at
t
with their latest observed state. If
g=0.34
we have “Prev. uncertain”, while if
g=0.99
we have “Prev. certain”.

Algorithm 2

Previous guess

{\mathbf{W}},{\mathbf{Y}}_{1:T},g

Initialize
\boldsymbol{\pi}^{g}_{n,0}
and
\boldsymbol{\pi}^{guess}_{n,0}
with
1_{M}\oslash M
for all
n\in[N]

for

t\in 1,\dots,T

do

for

n\in[N]

do

if

{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(M+1)}=0

then

\boldsymbol{\pi}^{g}_{n,t}={\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(1:M)}

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{guess}=g\odot{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(1:M)}+\frac{1-g}{M-1}\odot\left(1-{\mathbf{y}}_{n,t}^{(1:M)}\right)

else

\boldsymbol{\pi}^{g}_{n,t}=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{guess}

\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t}^{guess}=\boldsymbol{\pi}_{n,t-1}^{guess}

end

if

end

for

end

for

D.4.1
Changing the parameters values

Suppose now that we want to consider different parameters values, for instance we want
q_{R}=0
. This can be easily done by looking at the tutorial_SIR.ipynb file in the GitHub repository
LorenzoRimella/CAL
and by changing the configuration:

parameters = {
"prior_infection":tf.convert_to_tensor([1-0.5, 0.5, 0.0], dtype = tf.float32),
"log_beta":tf.math.log(tf.convert_to_tensor([3.0], dtype = tf.float32)),
"b_S":tf.convert_to_tensor([+0.5], dtype = tf.float32),
"b_I":tf.convert_to_tensor([+1.0], dtype = tf.float32),
"log_gamma":tf.math.log(tf.convert_to_tensor([0.05], dtype = tf.float32)),
"b_R":tf.convert_to_tensor([-0.1], dtype = tf.float32),
"log_phi":tf.math.log(tf.convert_to_tensor([1.5], dtype = tf.float32)),
"log_epsilon":tf.math.log(tf.convert_to_tensor([0.0001], dtype = tf.float32)),
"logit_prob_testing":logit(tf.convert_to_tensor([0.1, 0.2, 0.5],
dtype = tf.float32))
}

For the case
q_{R}=0
the above would change to:

parameters = {
"prior_infection":tf.convert_to_tensor([1-0.5, 0.5, 0.0], dtype = tf.float32),
"log_beta":tf.math.log(tf.convert_to_tensor([3.0], dtype = tf.float32)),
"b_S":tf.convert_to_tensor([+0.5], dtype = tf.float32),
"b_I":tf.convert_to_tensor([+1.0], dtype = tf.float32),
